# Known issues and limits

Numbered, never deleted: a fixed issue moves to "Closed" with the proof.

## Open

| # | issue | impact | plan |
|---|---|---|---|
| C745 | Found by a frozen test (N152 D2, M338), 25 Nov: Qt 6.10 sends the real characters typed in a password field (echo mode Password) in its AT-SPI text-changed events, 25/25 (GTK 3 sends masks, 0/25). While accessibility is on, any listener registered for that program receives them. Aurora's recorder drops them before storing anything (0/25 in its output, its draft and its memory), but they reach its process, and any other process of the owner may listen | during a demonstration only (accessibility is on only then); a risk for any desktop with accessibility on, not created by Aurora | the app says so during a recording («do not type passwords now»); accessibility stays on only while recording; to look at: whether Qt can be told to mask (upstream), and whether the recorder can stop listening to a program's text while a password field has the focus |
| C747 | Found by the owner (26 Nov): after a demonstration of a Zenodo upload nothing appeared in Aurora Desktop. The recorder had been started by hand, outside the app (no «Mostrami → Salva»), and wrote into a temporary folder emptied by the restart; the report said «what Aurora learned» about a recording that was never saved and held only the page map, not the steps (869 events, 1 focus, no field text: clicks from the browser extension are not announced as user gestures) | trust in what Aurora Desktop learned; the personal library stayed empty | every teaching goes through the app and ends in the personal library; a «Cosa ho imparato» page shows each saved procedure before anyone says it was learned; the browser needs real gestures or Aurora's own extension (phase 4); 26 Nov: the «Cosa ho imparato» page is in the app (desk/app/learned.py, every bench trial kept beside its draft); still open until a teaching goes through the app end to end |
| C751 | Found by me on the owner's question (27 Nov: «siamo sicuri che le immagini siano state correttamente processate?»): Evidentia's inventory runs OCR only on image files (jpg, png, tif); a **scanned page inside a PDF** gets pdftotext's text, which for a scan is none. Measured read-only on the corpus: 135 658 pages of «scan» or «mixed» PDFs with ≤ 20 characters (76 149 + 59 509), never searched. A sample of 30 of them, OCR'd by hand (Tesseract, 300 dpi, text not kept): 6 gave text, median 1.3 s a page; the others are photos or blank. The images of the DOJ collection (33 276) were read by OCR (92 nearly empty); none has a visual description, by design (photos of people never analysed), and the `images` table meant to classify them was never filled | 🟡 the owner's yes (27 Nov): evidentia/ocr_scans + script/evd_ocr_scans.py written and tested, running on the 135 658 pages; then the new passages' vectors (evd_embed) |

(The first diagnostic printed sqlite3.Row instead of the error: mine, fixed.) | install.sh installs tesseract-ocr and tesseract-ocr-eng, the Mac port tesseract (Homebrew); the test prints the error it gets | the next CI's cloud install |
| C761 | Found by the CI of 0.3.56 (27 Nov, the Mac install: «the question never reached the cloud step»), my regression from C746: on a machine without a local model (AURORA_LLM_BACKEND=cloud) nobody can ever look at a text, so every cloud question waited 60 s and was refused — a cloud-only installation answered nothing. The tests had a local model that always looked (conftest), so none saw it | there the fixed rules mask, as before C746, and the question goes; the wait stays where a local model exists but is down | test_mask_unseen::test_a_machine_without_a_local_model_masks_by_the_fixed_rules_and_answers; the Mac install in the next CI |

| C759 | Found by me on the first full run of 💰 Segui i soldi (27 Nov): its ties were written into findings.db while the OCR (C751) wrote it with 10 processes; SQLite refused the write at once («database is locked») twice, also with BEGIN IMMEDIATE and a 60 s timeout — a second long writer never gets its turn | the money's results live in their own money.db (findings' schema) beside the archive; corpus and findings are only read | full run 21,715 pages in 7.8 s while the OCR ran (M351); test_money.py |
| C741 | Found while designing N152 (draft 6), 24 Nov (read in the file's mode, the attack not tried): the owner's exemption from level B (sys/core/ethics/exemption.sig) is signed, but the file and its folder are writable by group aurora and its path is a setting: a revocation made by deleting it does not hold, since the service's account could put the old, still valid copy back. | the exemption and the private-cloud consent carry root's revocation number (/etc/aurora/revocations.json, root's: Aurora's account cannot write it), signed into their message; `sudo aurora-root revoke [--what private-cloud]` raises it and removes the file — every copy signed before stops verifying; number 0 keeps the message of before, so today's exemption holds | test_ethics::test_a_revoked_exemption_put_back_no_longer_counts |
| C748 | Seen by the owner (26 Nov, KDE «errore grave» notice): at the end of a bench run the bench's private copies of xdg-desktop-portal-kde (10) and ksecretd (1) aborted in 2 s, when the bench's nested screen went away before them; the owner's own portal and wallet were unaffected (same processes since boot), but the crash handler of his user showed the notice | closed 26 Nov as its own text says (the screen's name in the bus's activation environment before KWin starts); its row had stayed among the open ones | M340, test_desk_bench::test_the_screen_name_reaches_activated_services_before_kwin_starts |
| C749 | Found by the AI reviewer of N157 bozza 3 (27 Nov) and checked: the Android app's signing key and its password (sys/app/keys, owner:aurora 0640) are readable by the aurora user, and the app's sources and build.py are writable by the aurora group (2775/664): Aurora builds and signs her own app (the 📱 page offers the APK). An Aurora misled (a prompt injection) could change the app, sign it with the real key and offer it as an update; the phone would install it over the real one, same uid, its data and Keystore keys included | 27 Nov: the key 0600 (the owner's); the app's sources (sys/app/build.py, sys/app/android/**) under the owner's code signature, and build.py refuses to build when a source is not the signed one, or the tools (JDK, SDK), their folders or the key are not the builder's own or are writable by others — a program of the build cannot be swapped by Aurora's group. Live after `sudo aurora-root sign` and `chmod -R go-w sys/app/tools && chmod 700 sys/app/keys`. Residual, declared: an APK put in dist/ by Aurora's account cannot be signed with the real key, so no phone takes it as an update; a fresh install must check the signer (the 📱 page shows the sum) | test_app_build_trust (signed sources and own tools trusted; a changed source, a writable program, a readable key: refused) |

| C406 | Found by the owner, 27 Oct: two weeks of verdicts on the Security page and the alerts the same. The verdicts were only counted by the scorecard: no code used them, and the share of false alarms among the judged incidents stayed about half week after week (M261) (impact: the owner's time spent judging, with nothing learnt; the true alarms lost among the false) | sec_quiet: an alert judged false ≥ 3 times on one device with ≤ 20 % right proposed for quiet (per device since C407) (or quieted by herself at security 🚀), kept as low with no notification, woken by a 👍. 3 Nov (M274): the notified share false still 43 %, the owner's impression right; the cause and the fix in sec_autopilot and sec_quiet (a decline asked again after 10 more false alarms). To close: a week at 🚀 with the notifications down to about 2 a day and no 👍 on what she closed. Before: to close: a week after the owner's answers, the false alarms notified fall and no 👍 lands on a quiet kind. 31 Oct, three days in (M271): 25 incidents kept quiet, 27 of the 29 quiet judged false; no 👍 on a quiet pattern; the notified share false still 41 % (as before), three proposals unanswered. 9 Nov, five days at 🚀 (4–8 Nov): the alerts left to the owner 1.7 a day (10), no 👍 on what she closed — but the security pushes still 23–38 a day: 24 of the 31 of 8 Nov were her routine (a scanner blocked at Cloudflare every 30 min and its block lifted, the firewall's blocks lifted on time), pushed whatever the 🚀. Fixed: at 🚀 they are in the feed only (note(quiet=True)) and the digest counts the Cloudflare ones (tests/test_autopilot_routine.py). To close: a week from 9 Nov with the security pushes about 2 a day. **18 Nov, not reached:** the incident pushes were 1, 3, 7, 6, 9, 5, 16, 8 a day on 10–17 Nov, 6.9 a day on average (the push log). Matched to the security logs: 14 of the 55 are the Cloudflare blocks the owner asked to receive (C606, so the target of 2 a day no longer holds for them), 14 deny_burst, 9 ips_alert, 7 new_country, 3 new_port, 4 with no log line near, the rest single. **Owner, 18 Nov: deny_burst keeps its phone notification**, so the target must be restated before this can close **20 Nov, a cause of ours found (M309):** the verdicts were used (814 of the last 1,000 incidents closed by quiet patterns born of them, 62 by her judgement) but did not last: incidents.json keeps 1,000 incidents (about 11 days) and the verdicts left with them, so her judgement (10 per kind) closed 62 alerts on 9–13 Nov and none after; on 14–19 Nov 18 of the 31 alerts left to the owner were on known devices of kinds she had been closing, and the owner judged 15 of them false again. Fixed: sec_verdicts keeps the verdicts apart (90 days, 5,000), read by sec_quiet and sec_autopilot; her judgement is per signature (sec_quiet.what: an IPS alert by direction and port), no longer per kind; a quiet pattern never hides a high or critical alert; a «right» raises the same pattern's next alert one step and keeps it loud (sec_quiet.raised). tests/test_verdicts_c406.py. The verdicts before 9 Nov are lost: the ledger starts from the 28 left. To close: the target restated by the owner (deny_burst and the Cloudflare blocks keep their push since 18 Nov), then a week measured on it. **20 Nov, the owner's target:** at most 3 incident notifications a day, deny_burst and the Cloudflare blocks not counted; to close: a week measured on it after the restart that brings sec_verdicts. | 23 Nov, closed by the owner's decision on the measured numbers (M325): with sec_verdicts live, the incidents left loud with deny_burst and the Cloudflare blocks taken out were 4 on 21 Nov (2 egress_new, 2 ips_alert) and 4 on 22 Nov (3 new_country, 1 ips_alert), one over the target of 3; the incident pushes in all 9 and 11. | C672 | Found by the fifth referee, 20 Nov (reproduced): a plugin's secrets stayed in two environments inside its cage — AURORA_PLUGIN_SECRET_<KEY> (C463) in the plugin's own, which C659's re-exec did not clear, and the whole set in bubblewrap's, /proc/1/environ inside the cage — readable by any program the plugin runs (a git filter, C670), and the projects plugin has the network. | The GitHub token readable by code a project's configuration names. | Closed 20 Nov: a launcher (plg_launch, run by Aurora's own interpreter with -I) takes the secrets out of the environment and replaces itself with bubblewrap, which copies them from a pipe into a file inside the cage (--bind-data, nothing on the disk); the plugin reads it once (sys_config.plugin_secret) and empties it; for a plugin that writes the projects' folder (the projects plugin, where a caged command can plant code) a secret declared in its manifest's «env» is no longer put in the environment by its own name — the other plugins' programs still read theirs by name, and an «env_as» name an external program expects stays in its environment (a limit kept: their programs run no project code); the re-exec clears the prefix too. plg_host.py is PROTECTED: the owner signs. test_c672 (the real launch path with bwrap: neither /proc/1/environ nor /proc/self/environ holds the value, the file is empty after the read). |
| C671 | Found by the fifth referee, 20 Nov: the API's clone (api/projects.py) put the token on git's command line (-c http.extraHeader=…), readable by every process of the machine from /proc/<pid>/cmdline (/proc has no hidepid here, verified) — C659 had fixed the plugin's push only. Also: the push read the project's own configuration (url, http, credential keys refused one by one). | The token visible machine-wide during a clone. | Closed 20 Nov: prj_gitsafe.clone_command gives the header through GIT_CONFIG_COUNT/KEY/VALUE, with no system or user configuration, https only and no submodules; the plugin's push writes 'main' into a bundle, fetches it into a repository Aurora makes in a temporary folder with a configuration of her own, and pushes from there — nothing of the project's configuration comes along. test_c671, test_c670_the_push_never_reads_the_projects_configuration, test_c670_a_plain_repository_still_works_end_to_end (a push to a local bare repository). |
| C670 | Found by the fifth independent AI referee, 20 Nov (reproduced with the real cage and the real plugin) — IN PUBLISHED CODE, severity high: C658's list of dangerous keys was gone around five ways — (1) `git init --bare .` at the project's root with core.bare=false, core.worktree and a filter: no .git, so nothing was set aside nor refused, and git took the folder itself for the repository; (2) extensions.worktreeConfig with the filter in .git/config.worktree, and (3) .git/commondir pointing to another folder of the project — refusal() read .git/config only; (4) commit.gpgSign with gpg.format=ssh and gpg.ssh.defaultKeyCommand, run at «git commit», a key the list did not name; (5) a nested repository: «git add -A» made it a gitlink and «git status» ran its filter. With C672 the filter, running in the projects plugin's cage (which has the network), could read the token. | Code a caged command planted ran outside its cage. | Closed 20 Nov — the approach turned around: bubblewrap does not nest inside a plugin's cage (measured: «No permissions to create a new namespace»), so where git cannot be caged it runs only on a repository whose .git/config holds nothing but the keys git init, clone and remote add write (prj_gitsafe.ALLOW) — a key no list foresaw is refused too — and core.bare false; commondir, config.worktree, worktrees/, a .git file or link refuse it; for add, commit, status and the push's bundle also a nested repository and a .gitattributes asking for Git LFS (which would have committed the large files whole, without a word); the repository is named (GIT_DIR, GIT_WORK_TREE), never looked for, in the plugin, prj_gitsafe.run and prj_browse (caged and not); a second layer of -c overrides (commit.gpgSign=false, tag.gpgSign=false, safe.bareRepository=explicit, submodule.recurse=false, diff.ignoreSubmodules=all, push.recurseSubmodules=no…); project_publish refuses before the repository is created on GitHub. | C626 | Found by the second independent AI referee of N145/N146/C615/N147, 20 Nov (reproduced): the CPU and memory limits were the shell's ulimit, per process — six children used 6 s on 1 s declared; no process limit; the cage's /tmp (and the hidden folders' tmpfs) without a size: memory; with the GPU the address-space limit would stop CUDA and the local reasoner stayed on the GPU (law 6). | The whole run's CPU, memory and processes summed by Aurora over its process tree from /proc once a second (no per-run cgroup here: the services' account has no user manager); ulimit stays as a hard stop for a single process; processes a hard limit (ulimit -u counts in the cage's user namespace, measured: 38 forks with -u 40); /tmp a tmpfs of the memory declared (2 GB for ordinary commands), the hidden folders' tmpfs 64 KB; no core dumps; with the GPU no -v and the reasoner moved off and back.
